# Shadowdark Source Bible — Books 01, 02, and 03

## Scope and indexing rules

This index treats 01 as the core engine, 02 as the player-facing Western Reaches expansion, and 03 as the GM-facing companion. It is grouped by source book in precedence order, alphabetically within each book. A repeated name in different source books remains a separate record.

The index includes gameable headings (procedures, rules, options, spells, stat blocks, locations, factions, keyed entries) and separately adds named equipment/transport table entries and major city-state sites, districts, factions, and geographical features that are not all headings in the extracted Markdown. Generic contents/front-matter headings and repeated table column labels are omitted. Repetitive random table outcomes are represented by their parent procedure/table unless they are independent named entities.

**Page method:** the current printed page is updated by explicit page-ID anchors and by visible page separators of the form {N}---- in the extracted Markdown. This is why a section heading without its own HTML anchor may carry a later page number than its parent section heading.

The tag describes how a downstream DM or designer should treat the entry:
- **immutable** — changing it would break the core engine contract;
- **adaptable** — content may be reskinned or tuned while respecting its engine interface;
- **open** — the source leaves the element deliberately undefined or flexible;
- **conflicted** — two source statements genuinely contradict one another and neither is selected;
- **override** — a later/setting-specific source expressly replaces the default in a defined context.

No unresolved cross-book mechanical contradiction was identified. The Reaches travel procedure is tagged override because 03 expressly selects 02's alternative in that setting; it is not silently merged with 01's core overland cadence.

## 02 ↔ 03 matched player/GM material

| Player-facing entry in 02 | GM-facing cross-reference in 03 |
|---|---|
| The Bards | 03 city-state entry for City of Masks, Bardic College, and Master Poet Yeothin (03, pp. 17–18); GM city material expands the faction's local anchors. |
| The Jeweled Eye | Player faction is centered on Afarim Zarad and Alkesh; cross-reference the Alkesh city profile, The Suq, and local secretive/arcane powers (02, pp. 93–94; 03, pp. 19–20). No identically named 03 faction profile is established. |
| The Scarlet Bones | Player pirate faction is tied to seaborne/coastal activity; cross-reference Last Sea, Tal-Yool coastal sites, and relevant sea/coast encounter tables (02, pp. 95–96; 03, pp. 59–64, 69–70). No identically named 03 city-state faction profile is established. |
| The Society | Player faction's criminal influence can be cross-referenced with the City of Masks Thieves' Guild, House of Seren, The Shroud, and GM city intrigue (02, pp. 97–98; 03, pp. 17–18, 248–282). Treat as related criminal networks, not automatically identical organizations. |
| The Torchbearers | Player faction's exploration/treasure mission is supported by the GM campaign procedures, safe havens, rumor network, and keyed adventuring sites. Local tavern anchors are in the 02 faction city-presence tables (02, pp. 99–100; 03, pp. 30–36, 67–83). |
| The Wolves of Lydonia | Direct setting counterpart: 03 describes The Wolves of Lydonia as a Lydonian faction protecting civilization; cross-reference King Saren Sindalian, Lydonia, and the Sablewood (02, pp. 101–102; 03, pp. 25–26, 67–70). |

The six player factions in 02 are not assumed to be identical to every similarly themed GM organization. Exact matches are identified above; thematic or geographic relationships are labelled as related/indirect.

| d6 Details 1 Dud. The light source (even a magical one) goes out and needs to be replaced or recast. 2 Fire. The light source (even a magical one) flares and burns your hand. Take 1 damage. DC 18 DEX or drop it. 3 Air. A rogue wind or pocket of thin air blows through. DC 15 DEX or the flames go out. 4 Water. A sudden rain shower or dripping ceiling hits the light source. DC 15 DEX or the flames go out. 5 Earth. Falling rocks, mud, leaves, or ash cover the light source. DC 12 DEX to protect it. 6 Spark. If the light source is open flame, it sparks and catches on 1d4 flammable objects within close. | d100 Details Devil's Peak in the Rimespires has a mighty spell upon it No road in the Western Reaches is safe from local bandits Deep underground, there is a dark library full of knowledge If you see a suspicious mountain goat, try to capture it In the Steppes, the Iron King guards a castle full of treasure Pit fighting is a common practice in the Djurum Desert There is an inescapable prison-island in the Last Sea Rangers in the Sablewood are first to learn of dangers Portals to other worlds are hidden all throughout the land Demons hatch out of black trees in The Gloaming forest Giant crabs swarm all over a tropical island far to the south A secretive village of halflings hides in the Lowland Moor The founding king of Stonehall was not buried there Atop a cloudy peak, an immortal man dwells in isolation The dwarves in the Bastions are beset by wyvern attacks St. Terragnis fought mighty devils in the Rimespire peaks Black structures in the jungle house otherworldly beings The elf City-State of Lydonia is a surreal place of magic An accursed dragon flies over the desert during storms An ancient tyrant's castle lies abandoned in the Sablewood You might spot a rare blue tiger in the Dhalpurna range Elder dwarves called the dverg explored this land long ago The desert is home to ancient cults and dying traditions RUMORS | 03 | location | p. 49 | Geographic, settlement, district, hex, or keyed-site anchor; supports navigation, encounters, factions, rumors, and adventure placement. | open | — |

## Coverage notes

- Creature stat-block headings from 01 and 03 are indexed as creature records; the monster generator, stat attributes, monster roles, level bands, and mutation procedure are indexed as rules.
- Player class, ancestry, spell, deity, patron, faction, mount, boat, and warband material is indexed under its applicable type. Table-only equipment and transport names are supplemented as rows.
- The 03 Master Hex Key and regional/city headings are indexed as location records. Numbered hex-key headings in the regional section are classified as locations even when a keyed entry contains a monster or NPC encounter.
- The City of Masks map's named districts and principal sites are supplemented as rows where the source presents them in map tables rather than standalone headings.
- Rules and entities are not made mechanically more specific than the source. The one-line function statements are navigation notes for a DM or game designer, not paraphrases that add new rules.
